in (
5.2
) gives

\displaystyle A_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-A_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left|A_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-A_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{\left|(A_{i}-A_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}\right|}{\left\|g_{(-k+1)}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\left|(A_{i}-A_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{(-k+1)}\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}\right|}{\left\|g_{(-k+1)}\right\|_{2}}-\frac{\left|(A_{i}-A_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{\Delta}_{k}g_{(-k+1)}\right|}{\left\|g_{(-k+1)}\right\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\left|(\mathbf{e}_{i}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}\right|}{\left\|g_{(-k+1)}\right\|_{2}}-\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{k}\|_{\rm op}.

Since
g_{(-k+1)}
is independent of
\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}
, by recognizing that

(\mathbf{e}_{i}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}\mid\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2),

Lemma

27
gives that, for any
\delta>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|(\mathbf{e}_{i}-\mathbf{e}_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}\right|\geq\delta\sqrt{2/e}~~\big|~\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}\right\}\geq 1-\delta,

(B.61)

which also holds unconditionally. By invoking
Lemma

28
, we also have that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\|g_{(-k+1)}\|_{2}\leq 3\sqrt{r}\right\}\geq 1-e^{-2r}.

Together with (
B.59
), invoking condition (
B.54
) yields that, for any
k\leq j\leq r
,

\left|A_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-A_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(0)}\right|\geq\sqrt{2\over 9e}{\delta\over\sqrt{r}}-3\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{k-1}\geq\sqrt{1\over 9e}{\delta\over\sqrt{r}}

holds with probability at least
1-\delta-e^{-2r}
.

For any
1\leq j<k
, the same arguments can be applied except by using

(A_{i}-A_{j})^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{(-k+1)}\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}g_{(-k+1)}\mid\mathbf{O}_{(-k+1)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Finally, taking a union bound over
j\in[r]\setminus\{i\}
completes the proof.
∎

B.9
Proof of the results using the method of moments based initialization scheme

Our proof uses the following lemma which states concentration inequalities of the absolute value of the maximum of
N
independent standard normal variables.

Lemma 11
.

Let
W_{1},\ldots,W_{N}
be i.i.d. from
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Let
C>0
be some absolute constant. For any
\delta\geq 0
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i\in[N]}|W_{i}|<\sqrt{2\log(N)}-{\log\log(N)+C\over 2\sqrt{2\log(N)}}-\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta)}\right\}\leq\delta,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i\in[N]}|W_{i}|>\sqrt{2\log(2N)}+\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta)}\right\}\leq\delta.

Proof.

Its proof can be found in the proof of
Anandkumar et al. 2014b
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

8
.

Without loss of generality, we assume the identity permutation
\pi
and
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
.
In view of
Theorem

4
, we need to prove that with the desired probability, for each
k\in[r]
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\rm init}}^{(k)}(\mu,\nu)
holds for
\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}
with

\mu
and
\nu
given in (
5.10
). We prove this by induction.

Fix arbitrary
1\leq k\leq r
and suppose that
\mathcal{E}_{{\rm init}}^{(\ell)}(\mu,\nu)
holds for
\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{\ell}^{(0)}
for all
1\leq\ell\leq k-1
whenever
k\geq 2
. Recall from (
B.52
) that
\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})
quantifies the error of the previous
(k-1)
estimated columns. By
Theorem

3
, we know that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})\right\}\geq 1-(k-1)n^{-C},\qquad\text{with}\quad\varepsilon_{n}=C^{\prime}(\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}).

(B.62)

The sequence
\omega_{n}
is defined in (
4.2
).
Our goal is to show that

A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}\geq 1-C^{\prime\prime}r(\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}),

(B.63)

which also implies that

\displaystyle A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-\max_{\ell\in[r]\setminus\{k\}}A_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\geq A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-\sqrt{\|\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}\|_{2}^{2}-(A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)})^{2}}

\displaystyle=A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-\sqrt{1-(A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)})^{2}}

as desired.

Recall
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(k)}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)})
from (
5.6
) for any
i\in[N]

with singular values
\widehat{\sigma}^{(i,k)}_{1}\geq\widehat{\sigma}^{(i,k)}_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\widehat{\sigma}^{(i,k)}_{r}
and the leading left singular vector
\widehat{u}_{1}^{(i,k)}
. In particular, with

\widehat{i}_{*}:=\widehat{i}_{*}(k):=\argmax_{i\in[N]}\left(\widehat{\sigma}_{1}^{(i,k)}-\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(i,k)}\right),

the initialization for the
k
th round is

\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}=\widehat{u}_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}.

We write
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}:=\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(k)}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)})
for short in the sequel.
For notational simplicity, we write

\bar{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{A}.

(B.64)

With
\mathbf{P}_{k-1}^{\perp}
given by (
B.57
), we define the population level counterpart of
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{k-1}^{\perp}\left(\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}\bar{A}_{j}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}+\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{P}_{k-1}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k}^{r}\bar{A}_{j}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\left(\kappa\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}+\delta_{i}\right)

(B.65)

with singular values
\sigma^{(i,k)}_{1}\geq\sigma^{(i,k)}_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma^{(i,k)}_{r}
and the left leading singular vector
u_{1}^{(i,k)}
.
Here the scalar
\delta_{i}
is defined as

\delta_{i}={1\over 3}{\rm tr}\left(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})\right).

(B.66)

Note that
u_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}
can be treated as the population level counterpart of
\widehat{u}_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}
. The following error between
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}
and
\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}
is crucial in our analysis:

\Delta_{M,k}:=\max_{i\in[N]}~\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}.

(B.67)

For some sufficiently small constant
c>0
, define the following event

\mathcal{E}_{M,k}=\left\{\Delta_{M,k}\leq Cr(\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2})\sqrt{\log(N)}\right\}.

For future observation, we note that condition (
5.9
) and
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
ensures

\Delta_{M,k}\leq c\sqrt{\log(N)}.

(B.68)

Our proof of (
B.63
) consists of three main parts.

(i)

We argue that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
,
u_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}
must correspond to one column of
\{\bar{A}_{k},\ldots,\bar{A}_{r}\}
. Since
\{\bar{A}_{k},\ldots,\bar{A}_{r}\}
are orthonormal, this requires to establish a lower bound on the gap

\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2}>0.

(B.69)

(ii)

We apply a variant of the Davis-Kahan theorem to show (
B.63
) on the event
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
.

(iii)

We establish the upper bound of
\Delta_{M,k}
and show that
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
holds with high probability.

Proof of step (i).
To prove (
B.69
), define the population-level counterpart of
\widehat{i}_{*}
as

i_{*}:=i_{*}(k)=\argmax_{i\in[N]}\left(\sigma_{1}^{(i,k)}-\sigma_{2}^{(i,k)}\right).

(B.70)

By using Weyl’s inequality in
Lemma

34
twice, we find that

\displaystyle\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\sigma}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}-\widehat{\sigma}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2}-2\Delta_{M,k}

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\sigma}^{(i_{*},k)}_{1}-\widehat{\sigma}^{(i_{*},k)}_{2}-2\Delta_{M,k}

by definition of
\widehat{i}_{*}
in (
5.7
)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma^{(i_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(i_{*},k)}_{2}-4\Delta_{M,k}.

(B.71)

To further bound from below the right hand side, we introduce

i_{j}=\argmax_{i\in[N]}~\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j},\quad\text{ for all $j\in[r]$}.

(B.72)

Fix any
j\in[r]
and condition on
\bar{A}_{j}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}A_{j}
.
On the one hand, since
\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}
for
i\in[N]
are independent standard normal variables (see the argument in (
B.88
)), invoking
Lemma

11
gives that for any
\delta\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{i\in[N]}|\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}|<\sqrt{2\log(N)}-{\log\log(N)+C\over 2\sqrt{2\log(N)}}-\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta)}\right\}\leq\delta.

(B.73)

On the other hand, for any
i\in[N]
, since the index
i_{j}
in (
B.72
) is only defined through
\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}
, it is independent of
\bar{A}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{\ell}
, for all
\ell\neq j
, due to the orthogonality of columns in
\bar{\mathbf{A}}
. Thus
\bar{A}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i_{j})}\bar{A}_{\ell}
, for
k\leq\ell\leq r
with
\ell\neq j
, are
(r-k)
independent standard normal variables. Invoking
Lemma

11
again yields that for all
\delta\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{\ell\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\bar{A}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i_{j})}\bar{A}_{\ell}|\geq\sqrt{2\log(2r-2k)}+\sqrt{2\log(1/\delta)}\right\}\leq\delta.

(B.74)

Fix any
\delta\geq 0
. By choosing
N\geq 4r(r-k)/\delta^{2}
, combing (
B.73
) and (
B.74
) gives that with probability at least
1-\delta/r
,

|\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i_{j})}\bar{A}_{j}|-\max_{\ell\in[r]\setminus\{j\}}|\bar{A}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i_{j})}\bar{A}_{\ell}|~\geq~c\sqrt{\log(N)}.

(B.75)

From (
B.9
) and by the orthogonality of
\bar{\mathbf{A}}
, we observe that for each
i\in[N]
, the singular values
\sigma^{(i,k)}_{1}
and
\sigma^{(i,k)}_{2}
must take values from
\{\kappa|\bar{A}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{k}|+\delta_{i},\ldots,\kappa|\bar{A}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{r}|+\delta_{i}\}
. Invoking the definition of
i_{*}
in (
B.70
) thus gives that with probability at least
1-\delta/r
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{(i_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(i_{*},k)}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sigma^{(i_{j},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(i_{j},k)}_{2}

\displaystyle=|\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i_{j})}\bar{A}_{j}|-\max_{k\leq\ell\leq r,\ell\neq j}|\bar{A}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i_{j})}\bar{A}_{\ell}|

by (
B.75
)

\displaystyle\geq c\sqrt{\log(N)}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eigen_gap_lb_draft}}.

(B.76)

Since the index
j
above is chosen arbitrarily, in conjunction with (
B.9
), we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta/r
,

\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2}\geq c\sqrt{\log(N)}-4\Delta_{M,k}\overset{\eqref{bd_Delta_M}}{\geq}c^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(N)},

(B.77)

which proves (
B.69
).

Proof of step (ii).

From step (i), we may take
u_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}=\bar{A}_{k}
as we have already assumed
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
without loss of generality. Also recall
that
\widehat{u}_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}=\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}
.
An application of the Davis-Kahan theorem for rectangle matrices in
Lemma

36
yields that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-\delta/r
,

\displaystyle\sin\Theta\left(\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)},\bar{A}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\sin\Theta\left(\widehat{u}_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)},u_{1}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{2\bigl(2\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}+\Delta_{M,k}\bigr)\Delta_{M,k}\over(\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}+\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2})(\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2})}

by (
B.67
)

\displaystyle\leq{6\Delta_{M,k}\over\widehat{\sigma}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{1}-\widehat{\sigma}^{(\widehat{i}_{*},k)}_{2}-2\Delta_{M,k}}

by (
B.77
) and Weyl’s inequality

\displaystyle\leq{6\Delta_{M,k}\over\sigma^{(i_{*},k)}_{1}-\sigma^{(i_{*},k)}_{2}-4\Delta_{M,k}}

by (
B.9
)

\displaystyle\leq{6\Delta_{M,k}\over c^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(N)}}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eigen_gap_lb} and \eqref{bd_Delta_M}}.

(B.78)

The notation
\sin\Theta(u,v)
above operates
\sin
to the acute angle between any vectors
u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
and
v\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
. By further using the identity

\displaystyle\left[\sin\Theta(u,v)\right]^{2}=1-(u^{\top}v)^{2}={1\over 2}\|u-v\|_{2}^{2}

whenever
u^{\top}v>0
, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\delta/r
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}-\bar{A}_{k}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sin\Theta\left(\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)},\bar{A}_{k}\right)\leq{6\sqrt{2}\over c^{\prime}}{\Delta_{M,k}\over\sqrt{\log(N)}}

up to a sign, as well as

\displaystyle A_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}\overset{\eqref{def_bar_A}}{=}\bar{A}_{k}^{\top}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{1-\sin\Theta\left(\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)},\bar{A}_{k}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{1-{6\Delta_{M,k}\over c^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(N)}}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-{C\Delta_{M,k}\over\sqrt{\log(N)}}

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{bd_Delta_M}}.

This in conjunction with
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
implies (
B.63
).

Proof of step (iii).
In the third step we prove that
\mathcal{E}_{M,k}
holds with high probability. This is technically involved and deferred to
Lemma

12
of
Section

B.9.1
in which we prove that

\mathbb{P}\Bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{M,k}\cap\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})\Bigr\}\geq 1-n^{-C}-N^{-C}.

Finally, by collecting the results of steps (i) – (iii) as well as (
B.62
), we conclude that (
B.63
) holds with probability at least

1-kn^{-C}-N^{-C}-\delta/r.

Finally, taking the union bounds over
k\in[r]
completes the proof.
∎

Remark 12
.

Similar results of
Theorem

8
can be proved under the general setting in
Appendix

B
with
\mathbf{U}_{(r)}
replaced by
\widehat{\mathbf{Y}}
that satisfies model (
B.1
). Specifically, consider the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})
given by (
B.5
). For each
k\in[r]
,
Theorem

11
ensures that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\right\}\geq 1-n^{-C},\quad\text{with}~~\varepsilon_{n}=C\left(\eta_{n}+\sigma_{N}^{2}+\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}\right).

By repeating the same arguments in the proof of
Theorem

8
together with
Lemma

13
, we can verify that
the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\rm init}}^{(k)}
holds for
\mathbf{R}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{(0)}
with

\mu=1-C\left(\eta_{n}+\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}+\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}\right)r,\qquad\nu=2\mu-1

provided that condition (
5.9
) is replaced by
1-\mu\leq c^{\prime}
for some sufficiently small constant
c^{\prime}>0
.

B.9.1
The technical lemma that bounds
\Delta_{M,k}

By replacing
U_{t}
in (
5.6
) by
\widehat{Y}_{t}
, we have for each
i\in[N]
and
k\in[r]
,

\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}=\widehat{\mathbf{P}}_{k-1}^{\perp}\left({1\over 3n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\widehat{Y}_{t}\widehat{Y}_{t}^{\top}\widehat{Y}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\widehat{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{(i)}-\mathbf{G}_{(i)}^{\top}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{P}}_{k-1}^{\perp}.

(B.79)

With
\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}
defined in (
B.9
), the following lemma establishes the rate of
\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}
under the general setting in
Appendix

B
.

Lemma 12
.

Fix any
k\in[r]
. Let
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}
and
\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}
be defined in (
B.79
) and (
B.9
), respectively. On the event
\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})
with any
\varepsilon_{n}\leq 1/\sqrt{r}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-n^{-C}-N^{-C}

\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\left\{r\left(\eta_{n}+\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}\right)+\sqrt{r}\left(\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{k-1}+\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\right)\right\}\sqrt{\log(N)}.

Proof.

Pick any
k\in[r]
and
i\in[N]
. Start with

\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}+\|\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}

where we introduce the intermediate quantity

\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}=\widehat{\mathbf{P}}_{k-1}^{\perp}\left(\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}\bar{A}_{j}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}+\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{P}}_{k-1}^{\perp}

with
\delta_{i}
given by (
B.66
).
To bound
\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}
, recall that
\widehat{Y}_{t}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}
and
\bar{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{A}
. By writing

\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}=\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{\top},

the term
\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}
is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\left\|{1\over 3n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\widehat{Y}_{t}\widehat{Y}_{t}^{\top}\widehat{Y}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\widehat{Y}_{t}-\mathbf{G}_{(i)}-\mathbf{G}_{(i)}^{\top}-\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}\bar{A}_{j}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}-\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle=\left\|{1\over 3n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{t}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}\widetilde{Y}_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}-\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}A_{j}A_{j}^{\top}A_{j}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}A_{j}-\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq{\rm I}+{\rm II}

where

\displaystyle{\rm I}={1\over 3n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\widetilde{Y}_{t}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}\widetilde{Y}_{t}-Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}Y_{t}\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle{\rm II}=\left\|{1\over 3n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}Y_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}-\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}A_{j}A_{j}^{\top}A_{j}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}A_{j}-\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}.

Invoking
Lemma

14
together with (
B.5
) and (
B.18
) gives that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\bigl\{{\rm I}\lesssim rt\eta_{n}\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{F}\Bigr)\geq 1-6e^{-t^{2}}.

(B.80)

To bound from above
{\rm II}
, let
\otimes
denote the Kronecker product. In
1
we review some of the basic properties of the Kronecker product.
By using the notion

Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}Y_{t}=Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}{\rm vec}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)})

from part (4) of
1
,
we decompose it into two terms
{\rm II}\leq{\rm II}_{1}+{\rm II}_{2}
where

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\|{1\over 3n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}{\rm vec}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)})-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}\right]{\rm vec}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)})\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{1\over 3}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}\right]{\rm vec}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)})-\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}-\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}A_{j}A_{j}^{\top}A_{j}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}A_{j}-\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}.

Invoking
Lemma

23
gives that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm II}_{1}\geq Ct\sqrt{r^{3}\log(n)\over n}\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-t^{2}+\log(r)\right\}-r^{2}n^{-C}.

(B.81)

For
{\rm II}_{2}
, by using
Lemma

17
with
\mathbf{M}=\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}
, it is easy to verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}\right]{\rm vec}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)})

\displaystyle=3\kappa\sum_{i=1}^{r}A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}A_{i}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}A_{i}+{\rm tr}\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right)\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}+\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right)

so that, by (
B.66
),

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{1\over 3}\left\|{\rm tr}\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}(\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right\|_{\rm op}+{1\over 3}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}+\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\quad+{2\over 3}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}+\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}^{\top}\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\over 3}\left|{\rm tr}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)})+{\rm tr}(\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})\right|+2\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\|\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{(i)}\|_{\rm op}

by
\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\leq 1

\displaystyle={\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\over 3}\left|{\rm tr}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)})+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R})\right|+2\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\|\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\|_{\rm op}.

Classical concentration inequalities of i.i.d. standard normal variables ensure that for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\|_{\rm op}\geq 2\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{2t}\right\}\leq 2e^{-t},

(B.82)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm tr}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)})\geq\sqrt{2rt}\right\}\leq e^{-t};

(B.83)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm tr}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R})=\sum_{a,b=1}^{r}[\mathbf{G}_{(i)}]_{ab}[\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R}]_{ab}\geq\sqrt{2t\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R}\|_{F}^{2}}\right\}\leq e^{-t}.

(B.84)

By the fact that
\|\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R}\|_{F}^{2}=\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{F}^{2}\leq r\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}^{2}\leq r
, we conclude that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm II}_{2}\leq 4\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\sqrt{r}\left(t+1\right)\right\}\geq 1-4e^{-t^{2}}.

(B.85)

In conjunction with (
B.81
), we obtain that forall
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm II}\lesssim t_{1}\sqrt{r^{3}\log(n)\over n}+\left(t_{2}+1\right)\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\sqrt{r}\right\}\geq 1-e^{-t_{1}^{2}+\log(r)}-4e^{-t_{2}^{2}}.

(B.86)

It remains to bound
\|\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}
. We use some results already derived in
Section

B.8
. Recall from (
B.8.2
) that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})
,

\|\widehat{\mathbf{P}}_{k-1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{P}_{k-1}^{\perp}\|_{\rm op}\leq 3\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{k-1}.

It then follows that

\displaystyle\|\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Bigl\|\widehat{\mathbf{P}}_{k-1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{P}_{k-1}^{\perp}\Bigr\|_{\rm op}\Bigl\|\kappa\sum_{j=1}^{r}\bar{A}_{j}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}+\delta_{i}\mathbf{I}_{r}\Bigr\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq 6\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{k-1}\left(\kappa\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{r}\bar{A}_{j}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\bar{A}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\bar{A}_{j}\Bigr\|_{\rm op}+|\delta_{i}|\right).

Note that (
B.83
) and (
B.84
) imply

\mathbb{P}\left\{|\delta_{i}|\leq{1\over 3}\left|{\rm tr}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)})|+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{G}_{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R})\right|\leq{2\sqrt{2}\over 3}t\sqrt{r}\right\}\geq 1-2e^{-t^{2}}.

By also using
Lemma

13
,
we conclude that on
\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})
, for all
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{r}\left(t_{1}+t_{2}\sqrt{r}\right)

(B.87)

holds with probability at least
1-2e^{-t_{2}^{2}}-e^{-t_{1}^{2}+\log(r)}.

Collecting (
B.80
), (
B.86
) and (
B.87
) concludes that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{A}(k,\varepsilon_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{F}
, for all
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
,

\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{(i,k)}-\mathbf{M}_{(i,k)}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim t_{1}\varepsilon_{n}\sqrt{r(k-1)}+t_{2}r\eta_{n}+t_{1}\sqrt{r^{3}\log(n)\over n}+\left(t_{2}+1\right)\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\sqrt{r}

holds with probability at least

1-4e^{-t_{1}^{2}+\log(r)}-10e^{-t_{2}^{2}}-n^{-C^{\prime}}.

The proof is completed by using
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{F}^{c})\leq n^{-C}
, taking the union bounds over
i\in[N]
and choosing
t_{1}=t_{2}=C\sqrt{\log(N)}
.
∎

B.9.2
Other technical lemmas used in the proof of
Lemma

13

The following lemma bounds from above
\|\sum_{j=1}^{r}A_{j}A_{j}^{\top}A_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}A_{j}\|_{\rm op}
for
\mathbf{G}
being an
r\times r
matrix with i.i.d. entries of
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
contain i.i.d. entries of
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. For any fixed
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
, one has that for all
t\geq 0

\mathbb{P}\left\{\Bigl\|\sum_{j=1}^{r}A_{j}A_{j}^{\top}A_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}A_{j}\Bigr\|_{\rm op}\geq t\right\}\leq\exp\left(-t^{2}/2+\log r\right).

Proof.

Note that by using parts (4) and (3) of
1
, respectively,

A_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}A_{j}=(A_{j}\otimes A_{j})^{\top}{\rm vec}(\mathbf{G})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,(A_{j}\otimes A_{j})^{\top}(A_{j}\otimes A_{j}))=\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(B.88)

By
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
, we also know that
A_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{G}A_{j}
for
j\in[r]
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. The claim thus follows by an application of
Lemma

31
with
d=r
and

\nu=\|\sum_{j=1}^{r}A_{j}A_{j}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}=1.

∎

Lemma 14
.

Grant model (
B.1
) and
n\geq r^{2}\log(n)
. Let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
contain i.i.d. standard normal variables and
\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
be any orthogonal matrix. On the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{F}
given by (
B.5
) and (
B.18
) with
\eta_{n}\leq 1
, one has that for all
t\geq 0
,

\left\|{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\widetilde{Y}_{t}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}-Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{O}^{\top}Y_{t}\right)\right\|_{\rm op}\lesssim r\eta_{n}~t

holds with probability at least
1-6e^{-t^{2}}
.

Proof.

Let us write
\bar{\mathbf{G}}=\mathbf{O}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{O}^{\top}
. We bound from above

\displaystyle{\rm I}=\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over 3n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{2}\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\widetilde{Y}_{t}-(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t}\right].

Fix any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
. We bound from above

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{2}\left(\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\widetilde{Y}_{t}-Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t}\right)

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{2}

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}+q^{\top}Y_{t})(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t}-q^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t}.

Recall from (
B.4
) that
\widetilde{Y}_{t}=Y_{t}+\Omega_{t}
. We have

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq{2\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{2}|Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t}|+{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{2}|\Omega_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq{2\over n}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{4}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{n}(Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t})^{2}}+{1\over n}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{4}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{n}(\Omega_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t})^{2}}.

On the one hand, recall the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})
from (
B.5
) and
\mathcal{E}_{F}
from (
B.18
). By using (
B.33
), we have that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{F}
,

\displaystyle{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\widetilde{Y}_{t})^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}+{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\Omega_{t})^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa+4+C\sqrt{r^{2}\log(n)\over n}+\eta_{n}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1

\displaystyle\text{by $\eta_{n}\leq 1$, $r^{2}\log(n)\leq n$}.

(B.89)

On the other hand, since

Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t}=Y_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{O}^{\top}\Omega_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{2})
(see, for instance, the arguments in (
B.88
)), we have that for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{|Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t}|^{2}\geq 2t^{2}\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq 2e^{-t^{2}}.

(B.90)

Similar argument also yields that for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{|\Omega_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\Omega_{t}|^{2}\geq 2t^{2}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{4}\right\}\leq 2e^{-t^{2}}.

Finally, since

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{n}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\Omega_{it}^{2}\Omega_{jt}^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}\Omega_{it}^{4}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\Omega_{jt}^{4}\right)^{1/2}\leq r^{2}\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\Omega_{t})^{4}

(B.91)

and similarly

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{n}\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{4}\leq r^{2}\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4},

(B.92)

we conclude that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{F}
, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim t\left({1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{4}+{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim rt\left(\eta_{n}^{2}+{1\over n}\sqrt{\sum_{t}\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{4}}\sqrt{\sum_{t}\|\Omega_{t}\|_{2}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim rt\eta_{n}

(B.93)

with probability at least
1-4e^{-t^{2}}
.

We proceed to bound
{\rm I}_{2}
as

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{2\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left|(q^{\top}\Omega_{t})(q^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t}\right|+{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\Omega_{t})^{2}|Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq{1\over n}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\Omega_{t})^{4}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}(Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t})^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+{2\over n}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}\Omega_{t})^{4}\right)^{1/4}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}\right)^{1/4}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{n}(Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t})^{2}\right)^{1/2}

where we use Hölder’s inequality twice in the last step.
Similar to (
B.90
), we have for all
t\geq 0

\mathbb{P}\left\{|Y_{t}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{G}}Y_{t}|^{2}\geq 2t^{2}\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{4}\right\}\leq 2e^{-t^{2}}

so that in conjunction with (
B.92
), we have that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})\cap\mathcal{E}_{F}
, for all
t\geq 0
,

{\rm II}_{2}\lesssim rt\eta_{n}

with probability at least
1-2e^{-t^{2}}
. Together with (
B.9.2
), by observing that the arguments of bounding
{\rm I}_{1}
and
{\rm I}_{2}
hold uniformly over
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, the proof is complete.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of the decomposition of principal components and the results on estimating the loading matrix

Throughout the proof we write

\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}:={1\over n}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X},\qquad\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}:=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}]=\sigma^{2}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}+\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}

under Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
.

C.1
Proof of
Theorem

1
: the decomposition of principal components

We first state two lemmas that are used in the proof of
Theorem

1
. Their proofs are stated after the proof of
Theorem

1
.
The first lemma bounds from above
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}
in operator norm.

Lemma 15
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
, assume
n\geq r\log(n)
. For some large constant
C\geq 1
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim~\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}p\log(n)\over n}+{\epsilon^{2}p\log(n)\over n}+\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}\right)\right\}\geq 1-n^{-C}.

Lemma

15
together with
\omega_{n}\leq 1
ensures that
the event

\mathcal{E}_{X}=\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim~\sigma^{2}\omega_{n}\right\}

(C.1)

holds with probability
1-n^{-1}
. Recall that
\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\in\mathbb{O}_{p\times r}
contains the first
r
eigenvectors of
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}
. Further recall from (
4.1
) that

\mathbf{\Lambda}=\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}

with
\mathbf{L}\in\mathbb{O}_{p\times r}
containing the left
r
singular vectors.
The following lemma gives upper bounds of
\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}
for some
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
.

Lemma 16
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
, assume

\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})\geq C\left(\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\right)

(C.2)

for sufficiently large constant
C>0
.
On the event
\mathcal{E}_{X}
,
there exists some
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\lesssim~{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}.

Proof of
Theorem

1
.

We work on the event
\mathcal{E}_{X}
in (
C.1
) which, according to
Lemma

16
, ensures that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}

(C.3)

for some
\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
.
Write the
r\times r
matrix

\mathbf{M}:=\sigma~\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{1/2}.

By adding and subtracting terms, the matrix
\mathbf{U}_{(r)}
in (
2.2
) satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{(r)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{-1/2}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}

\displaystyle={1\over\sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}

\displaystyle={1\over\sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right){1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}

\displaystyle={1\over\sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{X}+{1\over\sigma}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{X}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right){1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{X}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right){1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{X}.

By further writing

\mathbf{X}^{\prime}:={1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{A}{1\over\sigma}\mathbf{Z}+{1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{E}:=bA{1\over\sigma}\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{N}

(C.4)

and

\begin{split}\mathbf{\Delta}&=\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\\
&\qquad+\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}\end{split}

(C.5)

we obtain

\mathbf{U}_{(r)}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}+\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\right).

(C.6)

Furthermore, we have

N_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}_{r}\left(0,\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right),\quad\text{for all }t\in[n],

with

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}=\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right\|_{\rm op}\leq{\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\leq{1\over c_{\Lambda}^{2}}.

(C.7)

In view of (
C.3
),
it remains to study the term
\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}
for bounding from above
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\rm op}
.
Since

\begin{split}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}&={1\over n}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\\
&=\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}+\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right)\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\\
&=\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\left(\sigma^{2}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{L}^{\top}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\right)\mathbf{V}_{(r)}+\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right)\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\\
&=\sigma^{2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{R}+\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{L}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})\\
&\quad+\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{R}+\sigma^{2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{L}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})\\
&\quad+\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right)\mathbf{V}_{(r)}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\mathbf{V}_{(r)},\end{split}

(C.8)

rearranging terms yields

\displaystyle{1\over\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}:=\mathbf{I}_{r}+{\rm\bf Rem}

(C.9)

where the remainder matrix
{\rm\bf Rem}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|{\rm\bf Rem}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}{\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{\Lambda})\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\left(2+\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}{\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{\Lambda})\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+{\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma^{2}\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+\epsilon^{2}\left\|\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{\omega_{n}\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}

\displaystyle\text{by $\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})\leq 1$}.

(C.10)

In the penultimate step, we used
\mathcal{E}_{X}
,
\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\leq 1
,
\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{\Lambda})=1
and
(
C.3
).
Let
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{r}
denote the eigenvalues of the left hand side in (
C.9
).
Since the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{M}
satisfies that

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}\left(\mathbf{M}\right)={1\over\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}},\qquad\text{for all $k\in[r]$.}

By Weyl’s inequality and (
C.9
), we further have

|1-\lambda_{k}|\leq\|{\rm\bf Rem}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})},\qquad\lambda_{k}\geq 1-\|{\rm\bf Rem}\|_{\rm op}\geq 1-C~{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\geq c

using
\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})\geq c_{\Lambda}
and
\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\leq c^{\prime}
.
The above two displays together with Weyl’s inequality yield

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle=\max_{k\in[r]}\left|{1\over\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}}-1\right|\leq\max_{k\in[r]}{|1-\lambda_{k}|\over\lambda_{k}}\lesssim{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}.

Finally, in view of (
C.5
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq{\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{1/2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}\|\mathbf{M}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{7/2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}{\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

C.1.1
Proof of
Lemma

15

Proof.

Assume
\sigma^{2}=1
for simplicity. By the identity

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}=\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}+\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E},

and using
\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{\Lambda})=1
, we find that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}-\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\right\|_{{\rm op}}+\left\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}\right\|_{{\rm op}}+{2\over n}\left\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}-\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\right\|_{{\rm op}}+\left\|\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{{\rm op}}+{2\over n}\left\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\right\|_{{\rm op}}.

Provided that
r\log(n)\leq n
, applying
Lemma

32
twice with

t=\sqrt{r\log(n)/n}
and
t=\sqrt{p\log(n)/n}
, separately, gives that, with probability at least
1-n^{-Cr}-n^{-C^{\prime}p}
, the first two terms in the right hand side of the above display are bounded from above by

\epsilon^{2}\left(\sqrt{p\log(n)\over n}+{p\log(n)\over n}\right)+\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}.

(C.11)

It remains to show that

\mathbb{P}\left\{{1\over n}\left\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\lesssim\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}p\log(n)\over n}\right\}\geq 1-n^{-C}.

When
p\log(n)\gtrsim n
, this follows by noting that

\displaystyle{1\over n}\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{1\over n}\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}{1\over n}\|\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\left\|{1\over n}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}\right)\left(\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\|_{\rm op}+\left\|{1\over n}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}-\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\right\|_{\rm op}\right)

together with the bounds in (
C.11
),
r\log(n)\lesssim n
and
\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\|_{\rm op}=1
.

When
p\log(n)\lesssim n
, we have

\displaystyle{1\over n}\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r},v\in\mathbb{S}^{p}}{1\over n}u^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}v

\displaystyle\leq\max_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(r),v\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(p)}{2\over n}u^{\top}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}v

\displaystyle=\max_{u\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(r),v\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(p)}{2\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}u^{\top}Z_{t}E_{t}^{\top}v

where
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(r)
is the
\varepsilon
-net of
\mathbb{S}^{r}
. Since
u^{\top}Z_{t}

is sub-Gaussian with sub-Gaussian constant
1
and
v^{\top}E_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\epsilon^{2}v^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}v
) with
v^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}v\leq\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}\|_{\rm op}=1
for all
1\leq t\leq n
, applying the Bernstein inequality (for instance,
[
68
, Corollary 5.17]
) together with union bounds over
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(r)
and
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(p)
yields that, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{{1\over n}\left\|\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\lesssim t\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}\over n}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\left|\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(r)\right|\left|\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(p)\right|2\exp\left\{-c\min\left({t^{2}\over n},{t\over\sqrt{n}}\right)n\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\exp\left\{-c\min\left({t^{2}\over n},{t\over\sqrt{n}}\right)n+(p+r)\log(3/\varepsilon)\right\}.

Taking
t=C\sqrt{p\log(n)}
for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
completes the proof.
∎

C.1.2
Proof of
Lemma

16

Proof.

We work on the event
\mathcal{E}_{X}
. Start with

\begin{split}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}&=\sigma^{2}\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\mathbf{I}_{p}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p})\\
&=\mathbf{L}\left(\sigma^{2}\mathbf{S}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{L}^{\top}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\mathbf{L}_{\perp}\mathbf{L}_{\perp}^{\top}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p})\end{split}

(C.12)

where we write
\mathbf{L}_{\perp}
such that
(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{L}_{\perp})\in\mathbb{O}_{p\times p}
. Write

\mathbf{W}:=\sigma^{2}\mathbf{L}\left(\mathbf{S}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{L}^{\top}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\mathbf{L}_{\perp}\mathbf{L}_{\perp}^{\top}

and observe that
\mathbf{L}
coincides with the first
r
eigenvectors of

\mathbf{W}
. Also note that

\lambda_{r}(\mathbf{W})=\sigma^{2}\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2},\qquad\lambda_{r+1}(\mathbf{W})=\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}.

An application of the Davis-Kahan theorem in
Lemma

35
thus yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\right\|_{{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq{2^{3/2}\over\sigma^{2}\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p})\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq{2\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\left(C\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}-\mathbf{I}_{p}\|_{\rm op}\right),

completing the proof.
∎

C.2
Proof of
Theorem

5
: upper bounds of the estimation error of the proposed PCA deflation varimax estimator

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
. Write

\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}:={1\over\sigma}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\widecheck{\mathbf{Q}},\quad\text{such that}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}={\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\over\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}}.

Recall that
\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}=1
. By adding and subtracting terms, we find

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}-\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}\right|\over\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{F}+{\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\right\|_{F}\over\|\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{\rm op}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|1-\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}\right|\sqrt{r}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\text{by }\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{F}\leq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}\sqrt{r}.

To bound the two terms on the right hand side of the above display, we work on the event that
\mathcal{E}_{X}
in (
C.1
) and either
Theorem

7
or
Theorem

8
holds.

Regarding the first term, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}={1\over\sigma}\|\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}=\left\|\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\left({1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{1/2}+\mathbf{S}\right\|_{\rm op}.

By
\|\mathbf{S}\|_{\rm op}=1
and Weyl’s inequality, we conclude

\left|1-\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}\right|~\leq\left\|{1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\|_{\rm op}.

Recall from (
C.9
) and (
C.1
) that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{X}
,

\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\left|1-\lambda_{k}\right|\lesssim\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}

where
\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{r}
are the eigenvalues of

\sigma^{-1}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}.

We obtain that

\left|1-\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{\rm op}\right|\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\left|1-\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\right|\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\left|1-\lambda_{k}\right|\lesssim\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2}.

(C.13)

For the second term
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}
, by adding and subtracting terms, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}

\displaystyle={1\over\sigma}\mathbf{V}_{(r)}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\widecheck{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}

by (
4.1
)

\displaystyle={1\over\sigma}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\widecheck{\mathbf{Q}}+{1\over\sigma}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}(\widecheck{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{A})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\left({1\over\sigma}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}{1\over\sigma}\|\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\|_{F}+{1\over\sigma}\|\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\|_{\rm op}\|\widecheck{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{S}\|_{F}\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\left|1-\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\right|

Note that (
C.13
) implies

{1\over\sigma}\|\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\|_{\rm op}~\leq{1\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}\left(1+\max_{1\leq k\leq r}\left|1-\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}\right|\right)\lesssim 1.

By further invoking (
C.3
) and
Theorem

7
(or
Theorem

8
) together with

\|\mathbf{S}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{r}
and
\|\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\|_{F}\leq\|\mathbf{D}_{(r)}^{1/2}\|_{\rm op}\sqrt{r}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}\lesssim(\omega_{n}+\epsilon^{2})\sqrt{r}.

Combining the above display with (
C.13
) completes the proof.
∎

C.3
Proof of
Theorem

6
: the minimax lower bounds of estimating the loading matrix

Proof.

By taking
\epsilon^{2}=0
, the lower bound
\sqrt{r^{2}/n}
is proved in
[
7
]
. It remains to prove the other lower bound
\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}p/n}
.

Since the result is invariant to
\sigma^{2}
, we assume
\sigma^{2}=1
without loss of generality. We fix any
\epsilon^{2}\geq 0
below and write
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}
for
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda},\epsilon}
.
Let
c^{\prime}>0
be some fixed, absolute constant. Write

\mathbf{D}=\diag(d_{1},\ldots,d_{r})

with

c^{\prime}\leq d_{j}\leq 1

for all
j\in[r]
. We fix the
\ell_{2}
-norm of columns in
\mathbf{\Lambda}
by considering the following subspace of
\mathcal{A}
:

\bar{\mathcal{A}}:=\bar{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbf{D})=\left\{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{A}:\|\Lambda_{i}\|_{2}=d_{i},\text{ for all }i\in[r]\right\}.

For any sequence
\beta_{n}>0
to be specified later, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\|\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}\geq{2\beta_{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle~\leq~\inf_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\inf_{\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\|\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\|_{F}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}\geq{2\beta_{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle~\leq~\inf_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}\geq\beta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{by }\bar{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle~\leq~\inf_{\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}\geq\beta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{by $\bar{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}$ again}.

It thus suffices to bound from below the first line in the above display. To this end, define the following parameter space

{\mathcal{L}}:=\left\{\mathbf{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}:\rank(\mathbf{L})=r,~\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{L})\geq c,~\|L_{i}\|_{2}=1,\text{ for all }i\in[r]\right\}.

Notice that

\mathcal{L}=\{\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{D}^{-1}:\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}\}.

We find that

\displaystyle\inf_{\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\|\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\|_{F}\geq 2\beta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\left\|\left(\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{D}^{-1}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{D}\right\|_{F}\geq 2\beta_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\sup_{\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{A}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\left\{\left\|\bar{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\mathbf{D}^{-1}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\right\|_{F}\geq{2\beta_{n}\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{D})}\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\widehat{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathcal{L}}\sup_{\mathbf{L}\in\mathcal{L}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{L}}\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{L}}-\mathbf{L}\|_{F}\geq{2\beta_{n}\over\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{D})}\right\}.

(C.14)

The parameter space
\mathcal{L}
is considered in
[
37
]
5
5

5

Although
[
37
]
did not put
\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{L})\geq c
in the parameter space, the set of hypotheses
\mathbf{D}_{0},\mathbf{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{D}_{L}
constructed in their proof does satisfy this constraint, see, for instance, display (47) and their upper bound for
\|\mathbf{D}_{l}-\mathbf{D}_{0}\|_{F}^{2}
in page 1509
. By adopting to our notation, invoking their Theorem III.1 with
\mathbf{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{x}=\mathbf{D}{\rm Cov}(Z)\mathbf{D}=\sigma^{2}\mathbf{D}^{2}
,
p=r
,
m=p
,
N=n
and
\sigma^{2}=\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}
gives

\inf_{\widehat{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathcal{L}~}\sup_{\mathbf{L}\in\mathcal{L}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widehat{\mathbf{L}}-\mathbf{L}\|_{F}^{2}\right]~\gtrsim~{\epsilon^{2}pr\over n\sigma_{1}^{2}(\mathbf{D})}.

In fact, by inspecting their proofs (displays (78) – (79) in conjunction with display (2.9) in
[
66
]
), we also have

\inf_{\widehat{\mathbf{L}}\in\mathcal{L}}\sup_{\mathbf{L}\in\mathcal{L}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\widehat{\mathbf{L}}-\mathbf{L}\|_{F}^{2}~\geq~c{\epsilon^{2}pr\over n\sigma_{1}^{2}(\mathbf{D})}\right\}\geq c^{\prime}.

for some absolute constants
c>0
and
c^{\prime}\in(0,1)
. In view of (
C.3
), we complete the proof by taking
\beta_{n}=\epsilon^{2}pr/(2n\sigma_{1}^{2}(\mathbf{D}))
and using
c\leq\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{D})\leq\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{D})\leq 1
.
∎

C.4
Proof of
Theorem

9
: the decomposition of improved low-dimensional representation

Proof.

We work on the event
\mathcal{E}_{X}
in (
C.1
). Recall that
d_{1}\geq\cdots\geq d_{p}
are eigenvalues of
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}
and
\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}
is the
j
th largest eigenvalue of
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}
for
j>r
.
By Weyl’s inequality, we have

\left|d_{j}-\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\right|\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\omega_{n},\quad\text{for all }j>r.

As a result, we have

\left|\widehat{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}-\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\right|\leq\max_{j>r}\left|d_{j}-\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\right|\lesssim\sigma^{2}\omega_{n}.

(C.15)

The rest of the proof largely follows from that of
Theorem

1
with slight modifications. First note that, due to
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{E}=\mathbf{I}_{p}
, (
C.3
) gets improved to

\|\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}~\lesssim{\omega_{n}\over\sigma_{r}^{2}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}.

Second, display (
C.8
) yields

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{D}_{(r)}-\widehat{\epsilon}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{R}+\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{L}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})+\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{R}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma^{2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{2}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{L}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})+\mathbf{V}_{(r)}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{X}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{X}\right)\mathbf{V}_{(r)}+(\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}-\widehat{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}})\mathbf{I}_{r}

so that

{1\over\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}=\mathbf{I}_{r}+\widehat{\rm\bf Rem}

(C.16)

where, by using the arguments of proving (
C.1
), we have

\|\widehat{\rm\bf Rem}\|_{\rm op}~\lesssim~{\omega_{n}\over\sigma_{r}^{3}(\mathbf{\Lambda})}+{|\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}-\widehat{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}|\over\sigma^{2}}\lesssim\omega_{n}.

(C.17)

Let
\widehat{\lambda}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\widehat{\lambda}_{r}
denote the eigenvalues of the left hand side of (
C.16
). Then for any
k\in[r]
,

|1-\widehat{\lambda}_{k}|~\lesssim\omega_{n},\qquad\widehat{\lambda}_{k}\geq 1-\omega_{n}\geq c.

Next, by writing

\widehat{\mathbf{M}}=\sigma\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{1/2},

we similarly have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{(r)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}+\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}\mathbf{X}^{\prime})

with
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Z}/\sigma+\mathbf{N}
and

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}&=\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{1/2}\\
&\qquad+\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{-1/2}\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{V}_{(r)}-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{R})^{\top}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{S}.\end{split}

(C.18)

To bound
\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}\|_{\rm op}
, similar arguments of bounding
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\rm op}
together with

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k\in[r]}{|1-\widehat{\lambda}_{k}|\over\widehat{\lambda}_{k}}\lesssim\omega_{n}

yield

\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Delta}}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\omega_{n}
, completing the proof.
∎

C.5
Proof of
Theorem

13
: guarantees of the improved deflation varimax rotation

Proof.

We first link any iterate
\{\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}\}_{\ell\geq 0}
in (
6.5
) to any iterate
\{\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}\}_{\ell\geq 0}
from

\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(\ell+1)}=P_{\mathbb{S}^{r}}\left(\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(\ell)}-\gamma~\widehat{\grad}F(\widetilde{Q}_{k}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})\right),\quad\text{for }\ \ell=0,1,2,\ldots

(C.19)

where, for any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
,

\widehat{\grad}F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}):=\grad F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})+\left(1+q^{\top}\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\mathbf{R}^{\top}q\right)\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\mathbf{R}^{\top}q.

Write

\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}:=\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\mathbf{R}^{\top}

for simplicity.
Suppose
\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}
for any
\ell\geq 0
. Recall that

\displaystyle\widehat{q}^{(\ell+1)}

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{1+\gamma^{2}\|\widehat{\grad}F(\widehat{q}^{(\ell)};\widehat{\mathbf{Y}})\|_{2}^{2}}}\left(\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}-\gamma~\widehat{\grad}F(\widehat{q}^{(\ell)};\widehat{\mathbf{Y}})\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}^{(\ell+1)}

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{1+\gamma^{2}\|\widehat{\grad}F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})\|_{2}^{2}}}\left(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}-\gamma~\widehat{\grad}F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})\right)

In the proof of
Lemma

1
, we have argued that

\grad F(\widehat{q}^{(\ell)};\widehat{\mathbf{Y}})=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\grad F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})

so that

\displaystyle\widehat{\grad}F(\widehat{q}^{(\ell)};\widehat{\mathbf{Y}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\grad F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})+\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}}^{\perp}\left(1+\widehat{q}^{(\ell)\top}\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\grad F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})+\mathbf{P}_{\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}}^{\perp}\left(1+\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}

by
\widehat{q}^{(\ell)}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\grad F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})+\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}}^{\perp}\left(1+\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}\right)\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)}

by (
B.21
)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{\grad}F(\widetilde{q}^{(\ell)};\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}).

This implies that
\widehat{q}^{(\ell+1)}=\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widetilde{q}^{(\ell+1)}
. Therefore, it suffices to analyze the solution obtained from (
C.19
). By inspecting the proof of
Theorem

11
, we see that the same arguments can be used provided that, for any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
,

\|\widehat{\grad}F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})-\grad h(q)\|_{2}~\lesssim~\eta_{n}+\alpha_{n}+\|\grad F(q;\mathbf{Y})-\grad f(q)\|_{2}.

(C.20)

Furthermore, the same arguments of proving Theorems
3
&
4
can be used with
\grad F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})
and
\grad F(q)
replaced by
\widehat{\grad}F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})
and
\widehat{\grad}F(q)
, respectively.

It remains to verify (
C.20
). Pick any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
. Recall
\grad f
and
\grad h
from (
B.15
) – (
B.16
). Observe that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\grad}F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})-\grad h(q)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\grad F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})-\grad f(q)\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\left(1+q^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q\right)\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q+\grad f(q)-\grad h(q)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\grad F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})-\grad f(q)\|_{2}+\left\|\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\left(1+q^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q\right)\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q-\left(1+\left\|q\right\|_{\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}}^{2}\right)\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}q\right\|_{2}.

In the proof of
Lemma

2
we have shown that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}(\eta_{n})
,

\|\grad F(q;\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}})-\grad f(q)\|_{2}\lesssim\eta_{n}+\|\grad F(q;\mathbf{Y})-\grad f(q)\|_{2}.

It remains to bound the second term from above by

\displaystyle\left\|\left(1+q^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q\right)\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q-\left(1+q^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}q\right)\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}q\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|q^{\top}\left(\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\right)q\right|\left\|\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}q\right\|_{2}+\left(1+q^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}q\right)\left\|\mathbf{P}_{q}^{\perp}(\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})q\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}-\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\right\|_{\rm op}\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\|_{\rm op}+2\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq 4\alpha_{n}

where in the last step we use the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathbf{\Sigma}}(\alpha_{n})
together with

\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\leq\alpha_{n}+\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\leq 2.

The proof is complete.
∎

C.6
Proof of
Theorem

10
: improved estimation of the loading matrix

Proof.

In view of
Theorem

13
, it suffices to show

\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\omega_{n}

on the event
\mathcal{E}_{X}
given by (
C.1
).
Start with the decomposition

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}^{-1}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{S}^{-2}\mathbf{R}

\displaystyle=\left(\widehat{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}-\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}^{-1}+\epsilon^{2}\left(\sigma^{2}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}^{-1}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-2}\mathbf{R}\right)

\displaystyle={1\over\sigma^{2}}\left(\widehat{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}-\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\right)\sigma^{2}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}^{-1}+\epsilon^{2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}^{-1}\left(\sigma^{2}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{R}\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)}^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{S}-\mathbf{I}_{r}\right)\mathbf{S}^{-1}\mathbf{R}.

Recall that (
C.16
) and (
C.17
) give

{\sigma_{r}(\widehat{\mathbf{D}}_{(r)})\over\sigma^{2}}\geq 1-\|\widehat{\rm\bf Rem}\|_{\rm op}\gtrsim 1-\omega_{n}\geq c.

Using (
C.15
), (
C.16
) and (
C.17
) yields

\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{N}-\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{R}\|_{\rm op}\lesssim\omega_{n}
, completing the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Concentration inequalities related with the
\ell_{4}
minimization

Recall from (
B.1
) that
\mathbf{Y}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{N}
with
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{Z}
and
\mathbf{N}
satisfying the specifications therein. Columns of
\mathbf{Y}
,
\mathbf{Z}
and
\mathbf{N}
are i.i.d. copies of the random vectors
Y,Z
and
N
, respectively.
Throughout this section, we write

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}:={\rm Cov}(Y)={\rm Cov}(Z)+{\rm Cov}(N)=\mathbf{I}_{r}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}.

(D.1)

D.1
A calculation of the 4th order moments of columns in
\mathbf{Y}

For any fixed
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, the following lemma computes the expectation of
Y^{\top}\mathbf{M}YYY^{\top}
under (
B.1
). In particular, it implies
f(q)=\mathbb{E}[F(q)]
with
f
and
F
given in (
B.2.1
) and (
B.12
), respectively.

Lemma 17
.

Grant model (
B.1
) with
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}
given by (
D.1
). For any fixed matrix
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\top}\mathbf{M}Y~YY^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=3\kappa\sum_{i=1}^{r}A_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}A_{i}A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top})\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}.

Furthermore, for any fixed
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, we have
f(q)=\mathbb{E}[F(q;\mathbf{Y})]
.

Proof.

We only need to prove the first statement as the second one follows immediately. Pick any
u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
.
By
Y=\mathbf{A}Z+N
, the independence between
Z
and
N
as well as
\mathbb{E}[Z]=0=\mathbb{E}[N]
, we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\top}\mathbf{M}Yu^{\top}YY^{\top}v\right]

\displaystyle={\rm I}+{\rm II}+{\rm III}+{\rm IV}

where

\displaystyle{\rm I}=\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}Zu^{\top}\mathbf{A}ZZ^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}v\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[N^{\top}\mathbf{M}Nu^{\top}NN^{\top}v\right]

\displaystyle{\rm II}=\mathbb{E}[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}Zu^{\top}NN^{\top}v]+\mathbb{E}[N^{\top}\mathbf{M}Nu^{\top}\mathbf{A}ZZ^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}v]

\displaystyle{\rm III}=\mathbb{E}[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}Nu^{\top}\mathbf{A}ZN^{\top}v]+\mathbb{E}[N^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}Zu^{\top}\mathbf{A}ZN^{\top}v]

\displaystyle{\rm IV}=\mathbb{E}[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}Nu^{\top}NZ^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}v]+\mathbb{E}[N^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}Zu^{\top}NZZ^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}v].

We proceed to calculate each term separately.

For
{\rm II}
, using
\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{O}_{r\times r}
,
\mathbb{E}[ZZ^{\top}]=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and
\mathbb{E}[NN^{\top}]=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}
from
1
and
3
gives that

{\rm II}=u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v~{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M})+u^{\top}v~{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}).

The terms
{\rm III}
and
{\rm IV}
can be calculated similarly. Indeed, since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}Nu^{\top}\mathbf{A}ZN^{\top}v]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}NN^{\top}vu^{\top}\mathbf{A}Z]=u^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v,

using analogous arguments gives

{\rm III}+{\rm IV}=u^{\top}\left(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\right)v.

Finally, to calculate
{\rm I}
, by writing
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}=\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}
,
u^{\prime}=\mathbf{A}^{\top}u
and
v^{\prime}=\mathbf{A}^{\top}v
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}Zu^{\top}\mathbf{A}ZZ^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}v\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}M_{ii}^{\prime}u_{i}^{\prime}v_{i}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{4}]+\sum_{i\neq j}M_{ii}^{\prime}u_{j}^{\prime}v_{j}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}]+\sum_{i\neq j}M_{ij}^{\prime}u_{i}^{\prime}v_{j}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}]+\sum_{i\neq j}M_{ij}^{\prime}u_{j}^{\prime}v_{i}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=3\kappa\sum_{i=1}^{r}A_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}A_{i}u^{\top}A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}v+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M})u^{\top}v+u^{\top}\mathbf{M}v+v^{\top}\mathbf{M}u.

In the last step, we used the excess kurtosis of
Z_{it}
in
1
.
By repeating the same arguments and by writing
\mathbf{M}^{\prime}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}^{1/2}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}^{1/2}
,
u^{\prime}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}^{1/2}u
and
v^{\prime}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}^{1/2}v
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[N^{\top}\mathbf{M}Nu^{\top}NN^{\top}v\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v+u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v+v^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}u,

implying that

\displaystyle{\rm I}

\displaystyle=3\kappa\sum_{i=1}^{r}A_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}A_{i}u^{\top}A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}v+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M})u^{\top}v+u^{\top}\mathbf{M}v+v^{\top}\mathbf{M}u

\displaystyle\quad+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v+u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v+v^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}u.

Combining the expressions of
{\rm I}
,
{\rm II}
,
{\rm III}
and
{\rm IV}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{\top}\mathbf{M}Yu^{\top}YY^{\top}v\right]

\displaystyle=3\kappa\sum_{i=1}^{r}A_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}A_{i}u^{\top}A_{i}A_{i}^{\top}v+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M})u^{\top}v+u^{\top}\mathbf{M}v+v^{\top}\mathbf{M}u

\displaystyle\quad+{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v+u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v+v^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}u

\displaystyle\quad+u^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}v~{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M})+u^{\top}v~{\rm tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})

\displaystyle\quad+u^{\top}\left(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\right)v

thereby completing the proof after rearranging terms.
∎

D.2
Concentration inequalities of quantities related with the 4th moments of
Y

In this section we state several important concentration inequalities related with the
4
th moments of columns in
\mathbf{Y}=(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times n}
. They in particular imply various controls of the Riemannian gradient
\|\grad F(q)-\grad f(q)\|_{2}
and the objective functions
|F(q)-f(q)|
. Throughout this section, with
\gamma_{Z}
defined in
1
, let

\sigma_{Y}^{2}=\gamma_{Z}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}

(D.2)

denote the sub-Gaussian constant of columns of
\mathbf{Y}
.

The following lemma bounds from above
\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]]\|_{2}
for any fixed
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, thereby providing a bound of
\left\|\grad F(q)-\grad f(q)\right\|_{2}
.

Lemma 18
.

Grant model (
B.1
). For any fixed
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-C}
,

\displaystyle{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}.

Proof.

Fix
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
. For all
t\in[n]
, we know that

Y_{t}
is sub-Gaussian with sub-Gaussian constant
\sigma_{Y}
given by (
D.2
).
Consider the event
\mathcal{E}=\bigcap_{t=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{t}
with
\mathcal{E}_{t}:=\mathcal{E}_{t1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{t2}
and

\mathcal{E}_{t1}=\left\{|q^{\top}Y_{t}|\leq 2\sigma_{Y}\sqrt{\log(n)}\right\},\quad\mathcal{E}_{t2}=\left\{\|Y_{t}\|_{2}\leq\sigma_{Y}\sqrt{r+4\log(n)}\right\}.

(D.3)

Invoking
Lemma

28
and taking the union bounds over
t\in[n]
ensure that

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-1/n
. (Note that this probability can be made
1-n^{-C}
for some large constant
C>0
with
\log(n)
in (
D.3
) replaced by
C\log(n)
).

In the rest of the proof, we work on the event
\mathcal{E}
and bound from above

{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}]\right]\right\|_{2}+{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c}\}]\right\|_{2}.

Note that the second term is no greater than

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[n]}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}u^{\top}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{6}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}]}~\max_{t\in[n]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c})}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sigma_{Y}^{4}\over\sqrt{n}}

(D.4)

where the last step invokes
Lemma

24
with
W=W_{t}
and
\sigma_{W}=\sigma_{Y}
. We proceed to apply the vector-valued Bernstein inequality in
Lemma

29
to bound the first term. Note that

\|(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}\|_{2}~\lesssim~\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{r+\log(n)}

almost surely. Further notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{6}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{12}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{4}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{8}~r.

The last step applies
Lemma

24
with
W=Y_{t}
,
\sigma_{W}=\sigma_{Y}
,
a=12
,
b=0
and uses

\mathbb{E}[\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{4}]=\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[Y_{it}^{2}Y_{jt}^{2}]\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{4}r^{2}.

(D.5)

Apply
Lemma

29
with
\sigma^{2}=nr\sigma_{Y}^{8}
,
U=\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{r+\log(n)}
and

t={1\over 6}\sqrt{\log(n)}(U+\sqrt{U^{2}+36\sigma^{2}})\asymp\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{nr\log(n)}

to obtain

{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}]\right]\right\|_{2}\leq\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}

with probability at least

1-28\exp\left(-{t^{2}/2\over nr\sigma_{Y}^{8}+\sigma_{Y}^{4}t\sqrt{r+\log(n)}/3}\right)\geq 1-n^{-C}.

In conjunction with (
D.2
), the proof is complete.
∎

The lemma below bounds from above
\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}])\|_{\rm op}
for any fixed
q,\bar{q}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
.

Lemma 19
.

Grant model (
B.1
). Assume
n\geq r
. For any fixed
q,\bar{q}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-C}
,

\displaystyle{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}\right]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}.

Proof.

Let
\otimes
denote the Kronecker product and define

H_{t}:=Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{2}},\quad\text{for each $t\in[n]$.}

In
1
we review some basic properties of the Kronecker product.
By using the fact that

q^{\top}Y_{t}~\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}=(q\otimes\bar{q})^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})=(q\otimes\bar{q})^{\top}H_{t}

(D.6)

from part (3) of
1
,
we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}\right]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[q^{\top}Y_{t}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes u)(H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}])(\bar{q}\otimes u).

Thus by Cauchy Schwarz inequality, it remains to bound from above

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes u)(H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}])(q\otimes u)

\displaystyle=\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left((q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left((q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}\right]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

To this end, we use a similar truncation argument as the proof of
Lemma

18
. Recall
\mathcal{E}_{t}
from (
D.3
). We work on
\cap_{t=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{t}
to bound from above

\displaystyle{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left((q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}\right]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\qquad+{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c}\}\right]\right\|_{\rm op}.

By similar argument in the proof of (
D.2
),
the second term is no greater than

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}]}\max_{t\in[n]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c})}~\lesssim~{\sigma_{Y}^{4}\over\sqrt{n}}.

(D.7)

We proceed to apply the matrix-valued Bernstein inequality in
Lemma

30
to bound the first term. Note that

\|(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}\|_{2}~\lesssim~\sigma_{Y}^{4}(r+\log(n))

almost surely. Further notice that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}^{c}\}\right]\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{4}\right]}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{8}(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{8}~r

\displaystyle\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem_moment} \& \eqref{bd_Y_four}}.

Thus, using
n\geq r
and applying
Lemma

30
with
d=r
,
\sigma^{2}=nr\sigma_{Y}^{8}
,
U=\sigma_{Y}^{4}(r+\log(n))
and

t=C\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{nr\log(n)}

yield

{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left((q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}Y_{t}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t}\}\right]\right)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}

with probability at least

1-14\exp\left(-{C^{2}\sigma_{Y}^{8}nr\log(n)\over\sigma_{Y}^{8}nr+\sigma_{Y}^{8}(r+\log(n))C\sqrt{nr\log(n)}}+\log(r)\right)\geq 1-n^{-C}.

In conjunction with (
D.2
), the proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma bounds from above
\sum_{t=1}^{n}((q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}])
uniformly over
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, thereby establishing uniform upper bounds of
|F(q)-f(q)|
.

Lemma 20
.

Under model (
B.1
), assume
n\geq r(r+\log n)
. With probability at least
1-n^{-1}
, we have

\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left((q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}\right]\right)\right|~\lesssim\sqrt{r^{2}\log(n)\over n}.

Proof.

Recall that

H_{t}:=Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{2}}

for each
t\in[n]
. Write its centered version as

\bar{H}_{t}=H_{t}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}].

(D.8)

By adding and subtracting terms, it is easy to verify that for any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
,

\displaystyle{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left\{(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes q)^{\top}\left(H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}]\right)(q\otimes q)

\displaystyle\leq{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes q)^{\top}\left(\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}]\right)(q\otimes q)+{2\over n}\left|(q\otimes q)^{\top}\mathbb{E}[H_{t}]\right|\left|\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes q)^{\top}\bar{H}_{t}\right|

so that it suffices to bound from above

\displaystyle{\rm I}={1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}]\right)\right\|_{\rm op},

\displaystyle{\rm II}=\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\left|(q\otimes q)^{\top}\mathbb{E}[H_{t}]\right|\left|\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes q)^{\top}\bar{H}_{t}\right|.

To bound term
{\rm II}
, recall
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}={\rm Cov}(Y_{t})
from (
D.1
).
By part (4) of
1
, we note that

\mathbb{E}[H_{t}]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}]={\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y})\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{2}}.

(D.9)

By further using part (3) of
1
and
