\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\|_{\rm op}\leq 1+\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}\leq 2
, we find

\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}(q\otimes q)^{\top}\mathbb{E}[H_{t}]=\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}(q\otimes q)^{\top}{\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y})=\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}q^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}q\leq 2.

Moreover, for any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
, we have

(q\otimes q)^{\top}\bar{H}_{t}=(q\otimes q)^{\top}\left(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y})\right)=q^{\top}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\right)q

by part (3) of
1
again.
The above two displays imply that

{\rm II}\leq\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{2\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}q^{\top}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\right)q=2\left\|\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}\right\|_{\rm op}.

By
n\geq r\log(n)
, invoking
Lemma

32
with
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{Y}^{\top}
,
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{G}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}
,
d=r
and
t=C\sqrt{r\log(n)}
gives that , with probability at least
1-2n^{-cr}
,

{\rm II}\lesssim\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}.

(D.10)

To bound the term
{\rm I}
, recall the event
\mathcal{E}_{t2}
from (
D.3
) and
\mathbb{P}(\cap_{t=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{t2})\geq 1-1/n
. Using part (3) of
1
gives the identity

\displaystyle\|H_{t}\|_{2}=\sqrt{(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})}=\sqrt{(Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t})\otimes(Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{t})}=\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.

(D.11)

We immediately have that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{t2}
,

\|\bar{H}_{t}\|_{2}\leq 2\|H_{t}\|_{2}\leq 2\sigma_{Y}^{2}(r+4\log(n))

(D.12)

almost surely. The rest of the proof works on the event
\cap_{t=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{t2}
to bound from above

\displaystyle{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}-\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}\right]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}+{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}^{c}\}\right]\right\|_{\rm op}.

(D.13)

The second term is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[(u^{\top}\bar{H}_{t})^{2}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}^{c}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[(u^{\top}\bar{H}_{t})^{4}\right]}\max_{t\in[n]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{t2}^{c})}

\displaystyle\lesssim~{\sigma_{Y}^{4}\over\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem_u_Ht_four}}.

(D.14)

To bound the first term in (
D.13
), we apply the matrix-valued Bernstein inequality in
Lemma

30
. From (
D.12
), we find that

\|\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|\bar{H}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}\leq\sigma_{Y}^{4}(d+4\log(n))^{2}

almost surely. Also note that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}]\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[(u^{\top}\bar{H}_{t})^{2}\|\bar{H}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}\bar{H}_{t})^{4}]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|\bar{H}_{t}\|_{2}^{4}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|H_{t}\|_{2}^{4}]}

\displaystyle\text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem_u_Ht_four}}.

Since

\mathbb{E}[\|H_{t}\|_{2}^{4}]\overset{\eqref{eq_Ht_Yt}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\|Y_{t}\|_{2}^{8}]=\sum_{i,j,k,\ell\in[r]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{it}^{2}Y_{jt}^{2}Y_{kt}^{2}Y_{\ell t}^{2}]\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{8}r^{4}.

(D.15)

we conclude that

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}\right]\right\|_{\rm op}\lesssim~\sigma_{Y}^{8}r^{2}.

Using
n\geq r^{2}
and applying
Lemma

30
with
d=r^{2}
,
U=4\sigma_{Y}^{4}(r+\log(n))^{2}
,
\sigma^{2}=C\sigma_{Y}^{8}nr^{2}
and
t=C\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{nr^{2}\log(n)}

gives

{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}-\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{H}_{t}\bar{H}_{t}^{\top}1\{\mathcal{E}_{t2}\}\right]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}\geq C\sigma_{Y}^{4}\sqrt{r^{2}\log(n)\over n}

with probability at most

14\exp\left(-{C^{2}\sigma_{Y}^{8}nr^{2}\log(n)\over C\sigma_{Y}^{8}nr^{2}+16C\sigma_{Y}^{8}(r+\log(n))^{2}\sqrt{nr^{2}\log(n)}}+2\log(r)\right)\leq n^{-C^{\prime}}.

Together with (
D.10
), the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma

20
can be used to bound from above
\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}]]\|_{2}
uniformly over
q,\bar{q}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
. This is stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 21
.

Under model (
B.1
), assume
n\geq r(r+\log n)
. With probability at least
1-n^{-C}
, one has

\sup_{q,\bar{q}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left\{(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}\right]\right\}\right\|_{2}\lesssim~\sqrt{r^{2}\log(n)\over n}.

Proof.

By definition, it suffices to bound from above

\Delta:=\sup_{q,\bar{q},q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left\{(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}q^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}q^{\prime}\right]\right\}.

By (
D.6
),
we have

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{q,\bar{q},q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q^{\prime}\otimes\bar{q})^{\top}\left(H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}]\right)(q\otimes q)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{q,\bar{q}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes\bar{q})^{\top}\left(H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}]\right)(q\otimes\bar{q}).

Finally, since using part (3) of
1
twice gives

\displaystyle(q\otimes\bar{q})^{\top}H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}(q\otimes\bar{q})

\displaystyle=(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(\bar{q}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}=(q\otimes q)^{\top}H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}(\bar{q}\otimes\bar{q}),

we conclude that

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(q\otimes q)^{\top}\left(H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[H_{t}H_{t}^{\top}]\right)(q\otimes q)

\displaystyle=\sup_{q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left\{(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}-\mathbb{E}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{4}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\text{by part (3) of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{fact_k_prod}}.

Invoking
Lemma

20
proves the claim.
∎

Applications of
Lemma

21
are used to establish the lipschitz continuity of
\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]]\|_{2}
for any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}

to further control
\|\grad F(\cdot)-\grad f(\cdot)\|_{2}
.

Lemma 22
.

Grant conditions in
Lemma

21
. Fix any
q_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
. With probability at least
1-n^{-C}
, the following holds for all
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
,

\displaystyle{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}+\|q-q_{0}\|_{2}\sqrt{r^{2}\log(n)\over n}.

Proof.

Pick any
q\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
.
Adding and subtracting terms gives

\displaystyle{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}\leq{\rm I}+{\rm II}+{\rm III}+{\rm IV}

where, by writing
\mathbf{\delta}=q-q_{0}
,

\displaystyle{\rm I}={1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle{\rm II}={1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(\mathbf{\delta}^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{\delta}^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle{\rm III}={3\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(\mathbf{\delta}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{\delta}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle{\rm IV}={3\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(\mathbf{\delta}^{\top}Y_{t})(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{\delta}^{\top}Y_{t})(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}.

Notice that
Lemma

18
can be used to bound from above the term
{\rm I}
as

\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm I}\lesssim\sqrt{r\log(n)\over n}\right\}\geq 1-n^{-C}.

For
{\rm II}
, we have that

{\rm II}\leq\|\mathbf{\delta}\|_{2}^{3}~\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{3}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}.

Similarly, we have

{\rm III}\leq\|\mathbf{\delta}\|_{2}^{2}~\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{3\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}

and

{\rm IV}\leq\|\mathbf{\delta}\|_{2}~\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}{3\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left[(u^{\top}Y_{t})(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[(u^{\top}Y_{t})(q_{0}^{\top}Y_{t})^{2}Y_{t}]\right]\right\|_{2}.

Invoking
Lemma

21
and using the fact that
\|\mathbf{\delta}\|_{2}\leq 2
gives that

\mathbb{P}\left\{{\rm II}+{\rm III}+{\rm IV}\lesssim\|\mathbf{\delta}\|_{2}\sqrt{r^{2}\log(n)\over n}\right\}\geq 1-3n^{-C},

completing the proof.
∎

The following lemma provides concentration inequality of
\sum_{t=1}^{n}Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}{\rm vec}(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top})
around its mean.

Lemma 23
.

Grant (
B.1
). Let
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
contain i.i.d. standard normal variables and
\mathbf{R}
be any
r\times r
orthogonal matrix that is independent of
\mathbf{G}
. Then for all
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-t^{2}+\log r)-r^{2}n^{-C}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}{\rm vec}(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top})-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}\right]{\rm vec}(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top})\right)\right\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\lesssim t\sqrt{nr^{3}\log(n)}.

Proof.

By conditioning on
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{Y}
, observe that

\displaystyle{\rm vec}(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{R}\otimes\mathbf{R}){\rm vec}(\mathbf{G})

by part (4) of
1

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}_{r^{2}}\left(0,(\mathbf{R}\otimes\mathbf{R})^{\top}(\mathbf{R}\otimes\mathbf{R})\right).

Since

(\mathbf{R}\otimes\mathbf{R})^{\top}(\mathbf{R}\otimes\mathbf{R})=(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})\otimes(\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R})=\mathbf{I}_{r}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{r}=\mathbf{I}_{r^{2}}

we conclude that
[\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top}]_{ab}
for
a,b\in[r]
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. By further noting that the quantity we want to bound equals to

{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{a,b=1}^{r}\bigl[\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\bigr]_{ab}~\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}]\right)\right\|_{\rm op},

applying
Lemma

31
with
d=r
,
\mathbf{A}_{k}=n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}]\right)
and

\nu={1\over n^{2}}\left\|\sum_{a,b=1}^{r}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}]\right)\right]^{2}\right\|_{\rm op}

yields that for all
t\geq 0
,

{1\over n}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}{\rm vec}(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top})-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t})^{\top}\right]{\rm vec}(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}^{\top})\right)\right\|_{\rm op}\geq t

with probability no greater than
r\exp[-t^{2}/(2\nu)]
.
Finally, by invoking
Lemma

19
with
q=\mathbf{e}_{a}
,
\bar{q}=\mathbf{e}_{b}
and taking the union bounds over
a,b\in[r]
, we have that with probability at least
1-r^{2}n^{-C}
,

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\leq{r^{2}\over n^{2}}\max_{a,b\in[r]}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}Y_{at}Y_{bt}]\right)\right\|_{\rm op}^{2}\lesssim~{r^{3}\log(n)\over n},

thereby completing the proof.
∎

D.3
Technical lemmas used in the proof of
Section

D.2

The following states simple higher moments bounds of sub-Gaussian random variables. We include it here for completness.

Lemma 24
.

Assume that
W\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
is a zero-mean, sub-Gaussian random vector with sub-Gaussian constant
\sigma_{W}
.
For any
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we have

\sup_{u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[|u^{\top}W|^{a}|v^{\top}W|^{b}\right]\leq C(a,b)\sigma_{W}^{a+b}

where
C(a,b)>0
is some absolute constant depending on
a
and
b
only.

Proof.

Pick any
u,v\in\mathbb{S}^{r}
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}\left[|u^{\top}W|^{a}|v^{\top}W|^{b}\right]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[|u^{\top}W|^{2a}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[|v^{\top}W|^{2b}\right]}.

Note that
u^{\top}W
is also sub-Gaussian with sub-Gaussian constant
\sigma_{W}
. This implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|u^{\top}W|^{2a}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(a)\sigma_{W}^{2a}.

Repeating the same argument for
\mathbb{E}[|v^{\top}W|^{2b}]
completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma contains the bound of the
4
th moment of the kronecker product between two sub-Gaussian random vectors.

Lemma 25
.

Let
W=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
where
\widetilde{W}
is a
\gamma
subGaussian random vector containing independent entries and
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}
is any symmetric semi-positive definite matrix. Assume
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}]=0
and
{\rm Cov}(\widetilde{W})=\mathbf{I}_{d}
. Then there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}(W\otimes W-\mathbb{E}[W\otimes W])\right)^{4}\right]\leq C\gamma^{8}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}\|_{\rm op}^{4}.

Proof.

For any
u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}
, we write
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
as the matrix such that
u={\rm vec}(\mathbf{U})
. By part (4) of
1
, we find that

W\otimes W=(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{W})\otimes(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\widetilde{W})=(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2})(\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}),

hence,

\displaystyle u^{\top}(W\otimes W-\mathbb{E}[W\otimes W])

\displaystyle=u^{\top}(W\otimes W-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}))

by (
D.9
)

\displaystyle=u^{\top}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2})\left(\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{I}_{d})\right)

by part (4) of
1

\displaystyle=u^{\top}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2})\left(\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}]\right)

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{exp_Ht}}.

Furthermore, we also have

u^{\top}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2})(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}^{1/2})^{\top}u=u^{\top}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W})u\leq\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}^{2}

where the last step uses part (6) of
1
. It then follows that

\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}(W\otimes W-\mathbb{E}[W\otimes W])\right)^{4}\right]\leq\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{W}\|_{\rm op}^{4}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}(\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}])\right)^{4}\right].

Note that part (3) of
1
gives

u^{\top}(\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}\otimes\widetilde{W})]=\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}].

An application of the Hanson-Wright inequality gives that, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}]\right|>t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left\{-c\min\left\{{t^{2}\over\gamma^{4}\|\mathbf{U}\|_{F}^{2}},~{t\over\gamma^{2}\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\rm op}}\right\}\right\}.

Together with
\|\mathbf{U}\|_{F}^{2}=\|u\|_{2}^{2}=1
and
\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|\mathbf{U}\|_{F}\leq 1
, we conclude that
(\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}])
is sub-Exponential with sub-Exponential parameters
C\gamma^{2}
for some absolute constant
C>0
. Therefore, for any
u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{W}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\widetilde{W}]\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim\gamma^{8}.

This completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma bounds from above the fourth moment of
u^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}])
for any fixed
u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}
and
t\in[n]
.

Lemma 26
.

Under model (
B.1
), there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\max_{t\in[n]}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}])\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim\sigma_{Y}^{4}.

Proof.

Recall
Y_{t}=\mathbf{A}Z_{t}+N_{t}
. For any
u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}
, using parts (1) and (2) of
1
gives

\displaystyle u^{\top}(Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{t}\otimes Y_{t}])

\displaystyle=u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t}+N_{t})\otimes(\mathbf{A}Z_{t}+N_{t})\right)-u^{\top}({\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{Y}))

by (
D.9
)

\displaystyle=u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\otimes(\mathbf{A}Z_{t})-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{I}_{r})\right)+u^{\top}\left(N_{t}\otimes N_{t}-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\otimes N_{t}\right)+u^{\top}\left(N_{t}\otimes(\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\right).

Note that
N_{t}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}^{1/2}\widetilde{N}_{t}
with
\widetilde{N}_{t}\sim N(0,\mathbf{I}_{r})
. Applying
Lemma

25
twice gives that

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[n]}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\otimes(\mathbf{A}Z_{t})-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{I}_{r})\right)\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim\gamma_{Z}^{4}

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[n]}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}\left(N_{t}\otimes N_{t}-{\rm vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N})\right)\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\|_{\rm op}^{4}.

Finally, it remains to bound from above

\max_{t\in[n]}\sup_{u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\otimes N_{t}\right)\right)^{4}\right].

To this end, recall that for any
u\in\mathbb{S}^{r^{2}}
we have
\mathbf{U}={\rm vec}(u)
and

u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\otimes N_{t}\right)=N_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A}Z_{t}.

Conditioning on
Z_{t}
, the fact that
N_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A}Z_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,Z_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A}Z_{t})
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{\top}\left((\mathbf{A}Z_{t})\otimes N_{t}\right)\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=3\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Z_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A}Z_{t}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=3\left(\mathbb{E}[Z_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A}Z_{t}]\right)^{2}+3{\rm Var}(Z_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A}Z_{t})

\displaystyle=3\left({\rm tr}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A})\right)^{2}+3{\rm tr}(\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{A})

\displaystyle=6.

The proof is complete.
∎

Appendix E
Auxiliary lemmas

E.1
Basic properties on the operation of kronecker product

The following fact contains some basic properties of the kronecker product.

Fact 1
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{B}
,
\mathbf{C}
and
\mathbf{D}
be commensurate matrices whose dimenions may vary across each property. Then

(1)

\mathbf{A}\otimes(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{C})=\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{C}
;

(2)

(\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{C})\otimes\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{C}\otimes\mathbf{A}
;

(3)

(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B})(\mathbf{C}\otimes\mathbf{D})=(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C})\otimes(\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D})
;

(4)

(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}){\rm vec}(\mathbf{M})={\rm vec}(\mathbf{B}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}^{\top})
with
{\rm vec}
being the vectorization operation which stacks columns of a given matrix.

(5)

There exists permutation matrices
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{Q}
such that
\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{A})\mathbf{Q}
.

(6)

\|\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}\|_{\rm op}=\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\rm op}\|\mathbf{B}\|_{\rm op}
. See, for instance,
[
42
]
.

E.2
Auxiliary deviation inequalities on random variables, vectors and matrices

Our proof has used the following anti-concentration inequality for standard Gaussian random variable, (see, for instance,
[
20
]
).

Lemma 27
.

Let
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{X^{2}\leq t^{2}\right\}\leq t\sqrt{e}.

The following lemma is the tail inequality for a quadratic form of sub-Gaussian random vectors. We refer to
Bing et al. 2021
for its proof.

Lemma 28
.

Let
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a
\gamma_{\xi}
sub-Gaussian random vector. Then, for all symmetric positive semi-definite matrices
H
, and all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\xi^{\top}H\xi>\gamma_{\xi}^{2}\left(\sqrt{{\rm tr}(H)}+\sqrt{2t\|H\|_{\rm op}}\right)^{2}\right\}\leq e^{-t}.

The following lemmas state the well-known vector-valued and matrix-valued Bernstein inequalities (see, for instance,
Minsker 2017
).

Lemma 29

(Vector-valued Bernstein inequality)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be independent random vectors with zero mean and
\max_{i\in[n]}\|X_{i}\|_{2}\leq U
almost surely. Denote
\sigma^{2}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\|X_{i}\|_{2}^{2}]
. Then for all
t\geq{1\over 6}(U+\sqrt{U^{2}+36\sigma^{2}})
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\right\|_{2}>t\right)\leq 28\exp\left(-{t^{2}/2\over\sigma^{2}+Ut/3}\right).

Lemma 30

(Matrix-valued Bernstein inequality)
.

Let
\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be independent, symmetric random matrices with zero mean and
\max_{i\in[n]}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{\rm op}\leq U
almost surely. Denote
\sigma^{2}:=\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{i}^{2}]\|_{\rm op}
. Then for all
t\geq{1\over 6}(U+\sqrt{U^{2}+36\sigma^{2}})
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|_{\rm op}>t\right)\leq 14\exp\left(-{t^{2}/2\over\sigma^{2}+Ut/3}+\log(d)\right).

The following lemma bounds from above the operator norm of the summation of a matrix Gaussian series, see,
Tropp et al. 2015
.

Lemma 31

(Matrix Gaussian Series)
.

Consider a finite sequence
\mathbf{A}_{k}
of fixed symmetric matrices with dimension
d
, and let
\gamma_{k}
be a finite sequence of independent standard normal variables. Introduce the matrix Gaussian series

\mathbf{G}=\sum_{k}\gamma_{k}\mathbf{A}_{k}.

Let
\nu(\mathbf{G})
be the matrix variance statistic of the sum:

\nu(\mathbf{G})=\|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{G}^{2}]\|_{\rm op}=\|\sum_{k}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{2}\|_{\rm op}.

Then for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\|\mathbf{G}\|_{\rm op}\geq t\right\}\leq d\exp\left\{-{t^{2}\over 2\nu(\mathbf{G})}\right\}.

Another useful concentration inequality of the operator norm of the random matrices with i.i.d. sub-Gaussian rows is stated in the following lemma
[
15
, Lemma 16]
. This is an immediate result of
Vershynin 2012
.

Lemma 32
.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be
n
by
d
matrix whose rows are i.i.d.
\gamma
sub-Gaussian random vectors with covariance matrix
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{G}
. Then, for every
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-ct^{2}}
,

\left\|{1\over n}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{\Sigma}_{G}\right\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\max\left\{\delta,\delta^{2}\right\}\left\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{G}\right\|_{{\rm op}},

with
\delta=C\sqrt{d/n}+t/\sqrt{n}
where
c,C
are positive constants depending on
\gamma
.

The following lemma provides an upper bound on the operator norm of
\mathbf{G}\mathbf{H}\mathbf{G}^{\top}
where
\mathbf{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is a random matrix and its rows are independent sub-Gaussian random vectors. It is proved in Lemma 22 of
[
15
]
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a
n\times d
matrix with rows that are independent
\gamma
sub-Gaussian random vectors with identity covariance matrix. Then, for all symmetric positive semi-definite matrices
\mathbf{H}
,

\mathbb{P}\left\{{1\over n}\|\mathbf{G}\mathbf{H}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\|_{{\rm op}}\leq\gamma^{2}\left(\sqrt{{\rm tr}(\mathbf{H})\over n}+\sqrt{6\|\mathbf{H}\|_{{\rm op}}}\right)^{2}\right\}\geq 1-e^{-n}

E.3
Auxiliary lemmas on matrix perturbation theory

We first state the Weyl’s inequality, widely used for perturbation on singular values of a given matrix
[
71
]
.

Lemma 34
.

Let
\mathbf{M},\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
be any matrix. The singular values of
\mathbf{M}
and
\mathbf{\Delta}
satisfy

\max_{1\leq k\leq\min(p,q)}|\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{\Delta})-\sigma_{k}(\mathbf{M})|\leq\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{\Delta}).

Here
\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{M})\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{\min(p,q)}(\mathbf{M})
are singular values of
\mathbf{M}
in non-increasing order.

The following is a variant of the well-known Davis-Kahan sin
\theta
theorem for symmetric matrices (see,
Yu et al. 2015
and also
[
23
,
58
]
). To state it, let
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{O}_{p\times r}
have orthonormal columns with principal angles defined as
(\cos^{-1}\sigma_{1},\ldots,\cos^{-1}\sigma_{d})^{\top}
where
\sigma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{d}
are the singular values of
\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{U}
. Let

\mathbf{\Theta}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V})=\diag(\cos^{-1}\sigma_{1},\ldots,\cos^{-1}\sigma_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}

and
\sin\mathbf{\Theta}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V})
be defined entrywise.

Lemma 35

(Davis-Kahan sin
\theta
theorem)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{\Sigma},\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
be symmetric with eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{p}
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\widehat{\lambda}_{p}
, respectively. Fix
1\leq r\leq s\leq\rank(\mathbf{A})
and assume that
\min(\lambda_{r-1}-\lambda_{r},\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{s+1})>0
, where we define
\lambda_{0}=\infty
and
\lambda_{p+1}=-\infty
. Let
d=s-r+1
, and let
\mathbf{V}=(v_{r},v_{r+1},\ldots,v_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=(\widehat{v}_{r},\widehat{v}_{r+1},\ldots,\widehat{v}_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
have orthonormal columns satisfying
\mathbf{\Sigma}v_{j}=\lambda_{j}v_{j}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\widehat{v}_{j}=\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\widehat{v}_{j}
for
j=r,r+1,\ldots,s
. Then

\|\sin\mathbf{\Theta}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}\leq{2\min(d^{1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{F})\over\min(\lambda_{r-1}-\lambda_{r},\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{s+1})}.

Moreover, there exists an orthogonal matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{d\times d}
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\widehat{\mathbf{O}}-\mathbf{V}\|_{F}\leq{2^{3/2}\min(d^{1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{F})\over\min(\lambda_{r-1}-\lambda_{r},\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{s+1})}.

The next lemma contains the Davis-Kahan sin
\theta
theorem for general rectangle matrices (see,
Yu et al. 2015
)

Lemma 36

(Davis-Kahan sin
\theta
theorem for general rectangle matrices)
.

Suppose that
\widehat{\mathbf{A}},\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
have singular values
\sigma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{\min(p,q)}
and
\widehat{\sigma}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\widehat{\sigma}_{\min(p,q)}
, respectively. Fix
1\leq r\leq s\leq\rank(\mathbf{A})
and assume that
\min(\sigma_{r-1}^{2}-\sigma_{r}^{2},\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{s+1}^{2})>0
, where we define
\sigma_{0}^{2}=\infty
and
\sigma_{\rank(\mathbf{A})+1}^{2}=-\infty
. Let
d=s-r+1
, and let
\mathbf{V}=(v_{r},v_{r+1},\ldots,v_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=(\widehat{v}_{r},\widehat{v}_{r+1},\ldots,\widehat{v}_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{q\times d}
have orthonormal columns satisfying
\mathbf{A}v_{j}=\sigma_{j}u_{j}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}\widehat{v}_{j}=\widehat{\sigma}_{j}\widehat{u}_{j}
for
j=r,r+1,\ldots,s
. Then

\|\sin\mathbf{\Theta}(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{F}\leq{2(2\sigma_{1}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\rm op})\min(d^{1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{F})\over\min(\sigma_{r-1}^{2}-\sigma_{r}^{2},\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{s+1}^{2})}.

Moreover, there exists an orthogonal matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{d\times d}
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}\widehat{\mathbf{O}}-\mathbf{V}\|_{F}\leq{2^{3/2}(2\sigma_{1}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\rm op})\min(d^{1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\rm op},\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{F})\over\min(\sigma_{r-1}^{2}-\sigma_{r}^{2},\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{s+1}^{2})}.
